# Requests to the Prior side from FJ00

**Written against:** fusion commit `e7c11fec` on branch `fj-json-workbench` (2026-09-28; the `codec/` tree is the one commit `c8992f13` landed), which read Prior at commit `12d8424`.
**Where the files are:** everything named here without a repository prefix lives under `codec/` in the fusion repository. Prior paths are marked `Prior:`.
**How to answer:** either edit the named fusion file, or write the ruling in a reply that names the fixture directory or the row; the fusion side applies a reply. Each request says which of the two it prefers.

FJ00 built the JSON schemas, the field-by-field mapping of Prior's three persisted aggregates (`candidates.Register`, `packages.Plan`, `campaign.State`) onto that contract, and a test set. Six things it cannot produce on its own are listed below, in the order they are needed.

## 1. Go-emitted goldens for the 13 round-trip cases under `codec/fixtures/prior/`

**Closes:** `_provenance` in every `codec/fixtures/prior/<aggregate>/<case>/prior.json`; the test gate `CODEC_REQUIRE_GOLDENS=1` in `codec/src/__tests__/prior-mapping.test.ts`.

A golden is the aggregate that Prior's own code builds in the named Go test, written by `encoding/json` from the Go value. Every `prior.json` today was derived by hand from the struct definitions and the test source, and says so: `"_provenance": "derived-from-source@12d8424"`. A hand-derived fixture can carry a `null` where Go writes `[]`, or a key order Go would not produce; the fusion side cannot tell.

| Case directory | Prior test |
|---|---|
| `register/duplicate-intake-and-source-change-require-requalification` | `Prior: modules/fusion/candidates/register_test.go` `TestDuplicateIntakeAndSourceChangeRequireRequalification` |
| `register/frozen-selection-replays-explanation-and-stale-prevents-admission` | same file, `TestFrozenSelectionReplaysExplanationAndStalePreventsAdmission` |
| `register/admission-persists-stable-item-and-current-attempt` | same file, `TestAdmissionPersistsStableItemAndCurrentAttempt` |
| `register/merge-chains-resolve-and-cycles-rollback` | same file, `TestMergeChainsResolveAndCyclesRollback` |
| `register/policy-records-every-exclusion-reason-and-closed-watermark` | same file, `TestPolicyRecordsEveryExclusionReasonAndClosedWatermark` |
| `plan/adaptive-formation-records-merge-split-and-deferral` | `Prior: modules/fusion/packages/packages_test.go` `TestAdaptiveFormationRecordsMergeSplitAndDeferral` |
| `plan/admission-crash-requires-observation-before-dispatch` | same file, `TestAdmissionCrashRequiresObservationBeforeDispatch` |
| `plan/dependency-cycle-is-deferred` | same file, `TestDependencyCycleIsDeferred` |
| `plan/failed-package-does-not-block-unrelated-package` | same file, `TestFailedPackageDoesNotBlockUnrelatedPackage` |
| `campaign/restart-cannot-reset-failures-reviews-or-resource-limits` | `Prior: modules/fusion/campaign/campaign_test.go` `TestRestartCannotResetFailuresReviewsOrResourceLimits` |
| `campaign/unknown-effect-and-baseline-deterioration-pause` | same file, `TestUnknownEffectAndBaselineDeteriorationPause` |
| `campaign/revocation-amendment-and-terminal-outcomes` | same file, `TestRevocationAmendmentAndTerminalOutcomes` |
| `campaign/discard-failed-and-cancelled-remain-distinct` | same file, `TestDiscardFailedAndCancelledRemainDistinct` |

What to emit, per case:

1. The aggregate as it stands at the end of the named test. If the current `prior.json` plainly matches an earlier point in the test, take that point instead and say so in `_provenance_note`.
2. The side inputs the test passed that the aggregate does not hold, under the key `_inputs`, marshalled by the same `encoding/json` call from the values the test used: `Policy` and `Snapshot` (the arguments of `Select`), `FormationPolicy` (the argument of `Form`), `CompletionEvidence` (the argument of `Finalize`), and `WorkItems` (the work item ids the test admitted, as a list of strings). Only the inputs the case used; the current files show which. The fusion test recomputes `SnapshotHash` from `_inputs.Snapshot` and `CharterHash` from `Charter`, so these must be the values that were hashed.
3. Three keys that are not Go fields, kept at the top of the file: `"_provenance": "go-golden@<Prior commit>"` (the fusion test accepts exactly `derived-from-source@<hex>` or `go-golden@<hex>`), `"_provenance_note"` (one sentence saying how the file was emitted), and `_inputs` as above. Everything else in the file is the Go value, key for key.

Whitespace does not matter: Prior stores the aggregates with `json.MarshalIndent(value, "", "  ")` (`Prior: modules/fusion/candidates/repository.go`, `packages/repository.go`, `campaign/repository.go`) and hashes them with `json.Marshal`, and the fusion test re-serialises before comparing. What matters is every value, the key order, and whether each empty collection is `null`, `[]` or `{}`.

Replace `prior.json` in the case directory; leave `fusion.json` alone, the fusion side regenerates it when the golden's values differ from the derived file. Preferred form: the edited files. Verification on the fusion side: `cd codec && CODEC_REQUIRE_GOLDENS=1 npm test` goes green only when all 13 files carry `go-golden@`.

On the shape of an emitter: a scratch Go program of about 150 lines exists on the fusion side (not in either repository) that reads a `prior.json`, drops the underscore keys, unmarshals the rest into Prior's structs, marshals it again with `encoding/json`, and recomputes `Revision`, `EvidenceHash`, `SnapshotHash` and `CharterHash` the way `registerRevision`, `planRevision`, `stateRevision`, `evidenceHash` and `charterHash` do. That proves the serialisation, not the scenario. A golden proper is written from inside the test, after its last step, by the same marshalling; a helper of a few lines in each `_test.go` file, or a `go run` program that replays the test's calls, is enough.

## 2. A ruling on every `confirmed: false` row in `codec/contract/prior-mapping.json`

**Closes:** the `confirmed` field of each of the 163 rows under `rows` and the 7 rows under `state_mapping.rows`. FJ04's migration refuses to run over an unconfirmed row, and never treats one as a default.

The request is a ruling per row, not a re-derivation: either `confirmed: true`, or a correction of the row's `fusion_path`, `type`, `null_vs_empty`, `revision_encoding` or `note`, with the reason in `note`. Preferred form: the edited file, or a reply listing `aggregate.prior_key` and the ruling.

**The seven `packages.Package.State` rows first.** The package itself is always kept verbatim under `campaign.formation.packages[]`; the row says which fusion package, if any, an importer creates in addition.

| `prior_value` | proposed `fusion_status` | proposed `outcome_class` | proposed note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `formed` | none | none | campaign formation only, no fusion package |
| `admitting` | none | none | campaign formation only, no fusion package |
| `admitted` | `open` | none | admitted but not started |
| `running` | `claimed` | none | needs a claim; checkout and person are not in the aggregate and must come from the migration plan, never be invented |
| `completed` | `done` | `completed` | without a bound evidence record the class is `legacy-completed` |
| `failed` | `dropped` | `failed` | `FailureReason` becomes the reason |
| `stale` | `paused` | none | reason `stale` |

Please also confirm that these seven values are the complete set a `Package.State` can hold at `12d8424`; an unknown value is refused at import, never reduced to `dropped`.

**Then the field rows, by aggregate.** Each row names the Go field (`prior_key`), the JSON path it lands on, the type there, and how an empty or zero value is treated (`null_vs_empty`, whose vocabulary the file's `conventions` block defines).

| Group | Structs (rows) | Rows to read with particular care |
|---|---|---|
| `candidates.*` (59) | `Source` (4), `Evidence` (2), `Candidate` (19), `Qualification` (6), `Disposition` (8), `Register` (7), `Predicate` (3), `Weight` (2), `Policy` (5), `Snapshot` (3) | `Register.Candidates` becomes one issue record per entry; `Candidate.Disposition` is split into `selection` and `admission`; `Candidate.ID` lands on `prior_id` and `Kind` on `item_kind`; `Statement` and `Purpose` land in `provenance.legacy_fields` (item 6); `Candidate.Version`, `Qualification.CandidateVersion` and `Disposition.CandidateVersion` refuse zero |
| `packages.*` (51) | `Candidate` (10), `FormationPolicy` (5), `Package` (13), `Deferred` (2), `Attempt` (4), `Admission` (6), `Plan` (11) | every Go map (`Plan.Candidates`, `Packages`, `Admissions`, `ActiveItems`) becomes an array of keyed entries sorted by key; `FormationPolicy.MaxRisk`, `MaxValidationCost`, `MaxSize` and `Attempt.Attempt` refuse zero; `Admission.Status` and `Package.State` are enums carried verbatim |
| `campaign.*` (53) | `Limits` (5), `Charter` (20), `Counters` (5), `Settlement` (4), `CompletionEvidence` (6), `State` (13) | `State.Charter` is hoisted to `campaign.charter`; `Charter.ItemKinds`, `CandidateSources` and `WorkflowTemplates` refuse nil and empty (`minItems 1`), the other lists accept both; `Limits.MaxAttempts`, `MaxConsecutiveFailures`, `MaxReviews` and `ResourceUnits` refuse zero; `State.State` and `RequestedOutcome` are enums carried verbatim |

Rows whose `null_vs_empty` is `nil-and-empty-to-[]` should be ruled on the corrected description in item 3c, not on the sentence the file carries today.

## 3. Four questions the spec leaves to Prior

**Closes:** the `_inputs` convention of item 1, the `nil-and-empty-to-[]` entry under `conventions.null_vs_empty` in `codec/contract/prior-mapping.json`, `provenance.legacy_fields.empty_collections` in every `fusion.json`, and the `WorkItemID` rule in `codec/src/prior/candidates.ts`. Preferred form: a reply.

**3a. Are `Policy`, `Snapshot`, `FormationPolicy` and `CompletionEvidence` persisted anywhere at `12d8424`?** The fusion side found them only as call inputs: `Select(ctx, registerID, snapshot Snapshot, policy Policy)` (`Prior: modules/fusion/candidates/register.go`), `Form(id, accepted string, candidates []Candidate, policy FormationPolicy)` (`packages/packages.go`), `Finalize(ctx, id string, evidence CompletionEvidence)` (`campaign/campaign.go`). Only `PolicyVersion`, `SnapshotHash`, `Plan.PolicyVersion` and the resulting `State` reach the aggregates, and no non-test file outside those three constructs the four types. That is why the fixtures carry them under `_inputs`, and why `register.policy`, `register.snapshot`, `formation.policy` and `state.completion_evidence` are `null` when a migration does not have them. If they are stored somewhere else (a collaboration state, a log, the `integration/` package), name the place and the fusion side reads them from there.

**3b. Do nil slices and maps reach disk as `null` or as `[]` / `{}`, aggregate by aggregate?** The fusion side found both forms in the code: `Open` creates `Candidates`, `Stable` and `Watermarks` as empty maps (`register.go`, `Register{ID: id, Candidates: map[string]Candidate{}, ...}`), `Form` creates `Candidates`, `Packages`, `Admissions` and `ActiveItems` as empty maps (`packages.go`), `Qualify` and `evaluate` create `Reasons` as `[]string{}` (`register.go`); whereas `Sessions`, `Runs`, `Candidate.Dependencies` and `Package.Dependencies` stay nil until something is appended and are written as `null`. `json.Unmarshal` keeps the difference on reload. Is the difference ever meaningful to Prior, or an accident of construction? Would Prior normalise it (which moves every stored revision), or is it stable at `12d8424`?

**3c. Is the `empty_collections` carry wanted?** Because both forms occur, the fusion import records under `provenance.legacy_fields.empty_collections` which collections were empty rather than `null`, and the export restores exactly that form, so the recomputed revision equals the stored one. The sentence in `prior-mapping.json` still says "export writes the Go nil form"; it is wrong and will be corrected to describe the carry (fusion issue `260928-1503_*_the-nil-and-empty-mapping-rule-says-export-writes-the-go-nil-form-and-that-cannot-round-trip.md`). The alternative is a round trip that is equal by value only, where nil and empty are the same and the revision may not match. Which do you want?

**3d. How is a `WorkItemID` resolved at migration?** `Disposition.WorkItemID` names a work item the register alone cannot resolve. The fusion side sees three candidates: `packages.Plan.ActiveItems` (item id to admission id), `packages.Attempt.ItemID` inside `Admission.Attempts`, or a store outside the three aggregates. Which one is authoritative, and what does an id that resolves nowhere mean (an admission crash before observation, or a corrupt register)? Today the import refuses such an id with `unresolved-reference` when the caller passes the set of known items, and carries it unchecked when it does not (`codec/src/prior/candidates.ts`).

## 4. A Go test on the Prior side over `codec/fixtures/manifest.json`

**Closes:** the FH01 acceptance sentence in `Prior: docs/design/fusion-dual-host-implementation-plan.md` (`### FH01`: "both adapters can validate the same fixture set; invalid fixtures are refused for named reasons"), and the open question in the FJ00 plan whether the Prior side accepts this manifest as the shared fixture index. Preferred form: the test in the Prior repository, plus a reply saying whether the fixture set is copied into Prior (stamped with the fusion commit) or read from a fusion checkout.

`manifest.json` is an array of 172 entries, one per file under `codec/fixtures/` except `prior/` and the two manifest files, validated by `codec/fixtures/manifest.schema.json`. Fields:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `path` | the fixture, relative to `codec/fixtures/`, forward slashes, no leading slash |
| `schema` | the `$id` of the schema to validate against (`urn:fusion:schema:fusion.workbench/v1`, `fusion.package/v1`, `fusion.record/v1`, `fusion.campaign/v1`, `fusion.evidence/v1`, each with the same prefix), or the literal `bytes` for a fixture that exercises the strict reader only |
| `expect` | `valid` or `invalid` |
| `error_class` | `schema-invalid` or `unsupported-format`; present exactly when `expect` is `invalid`; `unsupported-format` is the outcome for a `$id` the validator does not know (one fixture names `fusion.workbench/v2`) |
| `reason` | for a `bytes` fixture that is invalid, the strict reader's reason: `bom`, `too-large`, `invalid-utf8`, `duplicate-key`, `non-finite`, `multiple-values`, `not-an-object` or `syntax`; on any other entry it is documentation only |
| `note` | documentation, never asserted |

Counts at `e7c11fec`: 34 valid, 138 invalid (137 `schema-invalid`, 1 `unsupported-format`); by schema, record 54, package 46, campaign 24, evidence 20, workbench 20, `bytes` 8. Nine files sit under `bytes/`; the ninth, `crlf-only.bin`, is a valid workbench manifest with CRLF line endings and is listed under the workbench schema.

What the test asserts: for every JSON fixture, `expect` and, when invalid, `error_class`, and nothing else; the fusion harness never asserts a schema keyword or a message, and a manifest `reason` on a JSON fixture is not an assertion. For the eight `bytes` fixtures, the reader's `reason` as well. The strict reader refuses, in this order: more than 1 MiB (1 048 576 bytes), then a byte order mark, then invalid UTF-8, then the scanner's findings in document order (a duplicate key at any depth, a non-finite number token, a second top-level value, whichever comes first; the scan stops at the first token it cannot place, so a finding after such a token is not named), then a syntax error, then a top-level value that is not an object. `encoding/json` alone accepts a duplicate key (the last wins) and turns invalid UTF-8 into U+FFFD, so those two need a check of their own. The six schemas under `codec/schemas/` are JSON Schema draft 2020-12; every `$id` is `urn:fusion:schema:<namespace>` and cross-file `$ref`s use that URN, so all six are registered before any of them validates.

## 5. Three places where the spec and the fusion conventions disagree

**Closes:** for each, one row of `codec/contract/transitions.json` and the state enum in `codec/schemas/record.schema.json` on the fusion side, and the table in section 4.3 of `Prior: concept/fusion-json-workbench-spec.md` on the Prior side. The conventions cited are `rules/fusion-workbench-conventions.md` in the fusion repository, which the spec preserves by value. Preferred form: a reply per question.

**5a. Is `deferred → open` an edge on issues and plans?** Spec 4.3 maps the markers `_o_`, `_p_`, `_c_`, `_d_` onto `open`, `in_progress`, `closed`, `deferred` and says the import reads the existing transition rules per kind. The conventions (`## Terminal states are history`) make `_c_` and `_d_` terminal with no rename back: continuation is a new record citing the old one. Fusion's current reading: no such edge. `transitions.json` gives issues and plans five edges (`open → in_progress`, `open → closed`, `open → deferred`, `in_progress → closed`, `in_progress → deferred`) and makes `closed` and `deferred` terminal. If Prior needs a reopen (a deferred candidate selected again, say), say so; the answer changes `transitions.json` and `codec/src/__tests__/transitions.test.ts` here, and spec 4.3 there.

**5b. Which states does a discussion take?** Spec 4.3 gives discussions "the same four state values" as issues. The conventions (`## Filename Patterns`) give a discussion `_o_` and `_c_` only, and read an open one as interrupted, not pending. Fusion's current reading: `transitions.json` keeps the four values for imported records but allows the single edge `open → closed`; at FJ01 the enum should narrow to `open | closed`. The answer changes `record.schema.json`, `transitions.json`, the discussion fixtures under `codec/fixtures/valid/record/` and `invalid/record/` and `manifest.json` here, and the `discussion` row of spec 4.3 there.

**5c. What is the decision state behind `_d_`?** Spec 4.3 names it `dropped`. The conventions (`## State Markers — decisions`) name `_d_` Deferred: the user pushed the decision out, the record cites the deferral target and who ruled, and the state is terminal. Fusion's current reading: the schema and `transitions.json` carry the spec's `dropped` (edges `open → dropped`, `answered → dropped`), which is the wrong word, because `dropped` is also a package status and an outcome class and means something else there. The fusion side proposes `deferred` with a required target reference. The answer changes `record.schema.json`, `transitions.json`, `transitions.test.ts`, the decision fixtures and `manifest.json` here, and the `decision` row of spec 4.3 there.

**Closes:** `artefact_ref.kind` in `codec/schemas/common.schema.json`, `control.disposition.kind` for issues in `codec/schemas/record.schema.json`, and the two `candidates.Candidate` rows `Statement` and `Purpose` in `codec/contract/prior-mapping.json`. Both questions are filed as fusion decision records; cite them by these basenames. Preferred form: a reply, ahead of FJ01.

**6a. `260928-1420_*_which-closed-vocabularies-do-artefact-kind-and-issue-disposition-kind-take.md`.** Both fields are open lowercase tokens today, so they classify nothing. The record recommends closing `artefact_ref.kind` now from the artefact kinds fusion already names (`spec`, `plan`, `issue`, `decision`, `discussion`, `review`, `analysis`, `consultation`, `memo`, `forum`, `report`, `patch`, `log`, `other`), and closing an issue's `disposition.kind` once, with Prior, at FJ01, seeded from Prior's `Disposition.Outcome` set (`pending`, `selected`, `admitted`, `deferred`, `rejected`, `out_of_scope`, `merged`) and fusion's own resolution words (`fixed`, `duplicate`, `deferred`, `rejected`, `out-of-scope`, `merged`, `superseded`). Asked of Prior: which artefact kinds Prior's evidence and handover files need in the first list, and which disposition kinds Prior needs in the second. Any set adopted is additive-only afterwards.

**6b. `260928-1420_*_where-do-a-candidates-statement-and-purpose-live-after-import.md`.** The two prose fields belong in the issue's Markdown narrative under the spec's own principles; FJ00 also keeps them verbatim in `provenance.legacy_fields` so that the export reproduces Prior's bytes. The record recommends keeping that copy frozen at import for now, and asks Prior whether it can stop persisting the prose in the register and refer to the record instead, and when. A yes there removes the two rows from the mapping at FJ04 and the copy from provenance.

## FJ01

**Written against:** fusion commit `d9dff6ad` on branch `fj-json-workbench` (2026-09-28), which read Prior at commit `478ce21`. `codec/dist/fusion-record.js` at that commit is 440 232 bytes, `sha256:d9d0d440caeb7c311c1de942ca2471a3621dbcfb7d1bcefd6877c5922e4ff500`.

FJ01 turned the codec into one shipped file. `install.sh` now copies `codec/` into the install, and `codec/dist/fusion-record.js` is the one file an installed copy runs: plain `node`, no `node_modules`. `bin/fusion-record` is the Claude side's way in; the Prior side spawns the same file. The bundle reads one JSON request on stdin, writes one JSON response on stdout followed by a newline, and exits: 0 when a response was written, whatever its `ok`; 2 on a usage error; 3 when its inlined schemas do not load. Nothing is written to stderr except on 2 and 3. Four things are asked, in the order they are needed.

### 7. The Prior test adapter reads and updates the same record

**Closes:** the six pairs and `README.md` under `codec/fixtures/protocol-session/`, and the second half of FJ01's row in section 9 of `Prior: concept/fusion-json-workbench-spec.md` (read and updated from the Prior test adapter). Preferred form: the adapter and its test in the Prior repository, plus a reply carrying the revision and the record bytes from step 6 below.

What the adapter does, from an installed fusion copy:

1. Spawn `node <install>/codec/dist/fusion-record.js` with stdin and stdout pipes, one process per request. Take the executable digest of item 8 over the bundle file, not over `node`.
2. Copy `codec/fixtures/workbench/` to a fresh temporary directory, W, and use its absolute path.
3. Replay the six pairs in order, `01-show` to `06-validate`. In every `*.request.json`, replace the literal `<workbench>` with W before writing the bytes to stdin. In `06-validate.response.json`, the only answer that echoes the root, replace `<workbench>` with W before comparing.
4. Assert, per exchange, that stdout equals the recorded `*.response.json` byte for byte and that the exit code is 0. Two properties hold by design: `05-transition`'s stdout equals `02-transition`'s, because 05 repeats 02's `operation_id` and payload and receives the stored answer out of `W/.json-state/ops/`; and every revision in the six is the same on every machine, because a revision is `sha256:` over the stored bytes and the fixture bytes are fixed.
5. Perform one transition of the adapter's own: `transition` the same package from `claimed` to `paused`, with `expected_revision` set to the revision `03-show` returned, a fresh `operation_id`, an `actor` and a `reason`; expect `ok: true` and a new revision. `done` and `dropped` are the other two edges out of `claimed` in `codec/contract/transitions.json`; `done` needs an `outcome`. Note that FJ01's `transition` walks every package edge in that table, including `open -> claimed` and `claimed -> open`, which the table labels `claim` and `release`; those two become their own operations in FJ02.
6. Report the revision the adapter received and the bytes of `W/work-packages/260928-1200-parser-fix/package.json` after that write, so the fusion side can `show` them and compare.

Order matters: 02 writes what 03 reads and what 04 is refused against, and a copy on which 02 already ran answers 02 with the stored answer at once. Start from a fresh W for every run.

### 8. `node` on `PATH`, and the digest over the bundle

**Closes:** the sentence in the codec-port decision (`260928-1550_*_which-process-boundary-and-shipped-form-does-the-codec-take.md`, `## Recommendation`) that asks the Prior side to confirm both. Preferred form: a reply.

**8a.** Option 1 of that decision, ruled on 2026-09-28, means a Prior installation needs `node` (20 or later) wherever the Fusion module runs; Prior's core takes no Node requirement, only the module that spawns the codec does. Please confirm this is acceptable. If it is not, the protocol stays as it is and a per-platform single executable (option 2 of the decision) is a packaging change on the fusion side.

**8b.** What the fusion side sees in `Prior: internal/module/process.go` (lines 60 to 74 at `478ce21`): `StartProcess` opens `spec.Executable`, requires an absolute path to a regular file, hashes its bytes and compares `sha256:<hex>` with `spec.ExecutableDigest` before `exec.Command(spec.Executable, spec.Args...)`. Under option 1 the executable is the `node` binary and the bundle is an argument, so the check as written pins `node` and not the bundle. The bundle is a regular file whose first line is `#!/usr/bin/env node`, so it could be the executable itself; but `cmd.Env` holds only what the host lists, so `env` finds no `PATH` unless Prior passes one. Question: does the adapter take a second digest over `spec.Args[0]` beside the one over `node`, does Prior extend `ProcessSpec` with a digest over the bundle, or does it want the bundle to be the executable with a `PATH` entry in the explicit environment? The fusion side assumes none of the three.

### 9. The framing on the pipes from FJ02 on

**Closes:** the open question at the foot of the FJ01 plan (`260928-1550_*_plan-fj01-codec-port-bundle-wrapper-and-first-record-round-trip.md`, `## Open Questions`). Preferred form: a reply, ahead of FJ02.

FJ01 speaks one request, one response, then exit, and needs no framing. What the fusion side can see of Prior's alternative, in `Prior: moduleapi/v1/frame.go` and `protocol.go`: a frame is a 4-byte big-endian length followed by a JSON object with `schema_version`, `message_id`, `kind`, `correlation_id` and `payload`, at most 1 MiB; a connection opens with a `module.handshake` frame and expects `module.ready` back, authenticated with an HMAC over a secret the host passes in `PRIOR_MODULE_CONNECTION_SECRET`; the process is started with `Setpgid` and killed as a group on close. Which does Prior want for a long-running codec process: (a) `moduleapi` framing, one `fusion-record` request per frame payload, the handshake included; or (b) newline-delimited JSON, one request per line and one response per line, with any framing done by Prior's adapter around the process? A third answer, one process per request as in FJ01, is also acceptable to the fusion side; say so if it is enough.

### 10. The nine deferred operations are answered, not broken

**Closes:** nothing to edit; the adapter's reading of `codec/schemas/protocol.schema.json` (its `description`) and `codec/src/cli/ops.ts` lines 91, 112 and 294. Preferred form: none needed.

The protocol schema names fourteen operations. FJ01 implements `inspect`, `list`, `show`, `validate` and `transition`; the other nine (`create`, `claim`, `release`, `set-mode`, `set-dependencies`, `adopt-plan`, `attach-evidence`, `reconcile`, `migration`) are validated against the schema and then answered `{"ok":false,"error":{"class":"operation-unknown","reason":"not-implemented-in-fj01","detail":"..."}}` on stdout with exit 0. A `transition` on an issue, decision or any other non-package record receives the same class and reason. An adapter must read that answer as "lands in a later package", not as a codec defect. For contrast: an `op` outside the fourteen is `operation-unknown/unknown-op`, and a request that is not strict JSON or fails the schema is `schema-invalid`, both also on stdout with exit 0.

## FJ01b (the FJ00 follow-up)

**Written against:** fusion commit `dc798133` on branch `fj-json-workbench` (2026-09-28), which read Prior at commit `c512c4c` (the two response documents and the ruling equal the blobs at that commit). `codec/dist/fusion-record.js` at that commit is 445 664 bytes, `sha256:f82aa558444a85e6ddf99ed8ba16346832a27e27207e00930d002c74a1727230`. The bundle took those bytes at `451e771c` (step 7, the last commit that moved an inlined schema or table); the two commits after it added a test suite and README text and moved no inlined file.

FJ01b applied, in `codec/`, everything the Prior side ruled on FJ00's six requests and FJ01's four, and froze the result at one revision. **Written against:** fusion commit `72a7051b` on branch `fj-json-workbench` (2026-09-29), which read Prior at commit `590dca5` (the FJ01b response as committed at `f18481c`, unchanged since). `codec/dist/fusion-record.js` at that commit is 511 869 bytes, `sha256:9048e883e1a522597f782224cff7c1d444cc21ec5348c26dd9916fed68f3cc02`. The bundle took those bytes at `d1a646d7` (step 8, the last commit that moved code or an inlined file); step 9 added a test suite, the recorded session and README text and moved no inlined file.

FJ02 builds on the contract Prior accepted in `Prior: docs/design/fusion-fj01b-prior-response.md` ("FJ02 may build on the agreed `dc798133` contract"), keeps its two standing decisions (one codec process per request; common authority, ownership and CAS rules for `transition`, `claim` and `release`), and meets the sentence `Prior: docs/design/prior-installed-module-bundles.md` `## Remaining integration` binds it to: "FJ02 remains responsible for its operation kernel/journal; the next record-service integration must use that shared codec, not introduce a competing writer for Fusion JSON". `codec/src/kernel.ts` is that one writer; FJ01's `writeControl`, the only other write path, is gone. The commits, in order: `e19e5abe` (step 1, the schema widenings and their fixtures), `20942d0c` (2, the journal, the workbench write lock, the self-ignoring `.json-state/`), `ddb2f304` (3, the kernel, the read protocol, `transition` over every kind), `6225a8cb` (4, `claim`, `release`, `set-mode`), `23caa25e` (5, `create`), `508633ec` (6, `set-dependencies`, `adopt-plan`), `cc3d82b0` (7, `attach-evidence` and evidence records), `d1a646d7` (8, `reconcile`), `72a7051b` (9, the recorded session and the codec README). At `72a7051b`, `cd codec && CODEC_REQUIRE_GOLDENS=1 npm test` is green: 16 test files, 955 tests, `fixtures: 218 manifest entries`, `0 derived-from-source, 13 Go-emitted golden(s)`, `163 of 163 prior_keys exercised`, `round-trip-cli.test.ts` and `prior-handback.test.ts` without regeneration. `codec/README.md` (`## The CLI`, `## The kernel and the journal`, `## Evidence records on disk`, `## What reconcile reports`) is the full statement; what follows is what the Prior side needs from it.

### What landed

1. **Every operation answers but `migration`.** `inspect` reports `operations.implemented` as the thirteen others and `operations.deferred` as `["migration"]`, and `kinds` gains `evidence`. `migration` answers `{"ok":false,"error":{"class":"operation-unknown","reason":"not-implemented","detail":"migration is specified (spec section 6) and lands in FJ04"}}`, exit 0. The reason token changed at step 3 from `not-implemented-in-fj01` to `not-implemented` and is stable from here: a token that names a package goes stale at every package, so the package moved into the detail. Prior's adapter keys on the class (`Prior: internal/fusionhost/codec_process.go` line 139), so the token change moves nothing in it; the one pin on the reason, `TestCodecPinsScopeAndDomainRefusals` (`Prior: internal/fusionhost/codec_process_test.go` lines 252 to 263), sends `reconcile`, which now answers `ok: true`, so that assertion turns red at the re-pin whatever the token (request 17).
2. **One reading of FJ01's item 10 no longer holds on its own.** `operation-unknown` meant "lands in a later package". From FJ02 the class carries three reasons, and a host tells them apart by the reason: `not-implemented` (lands later; `migration` only), `unknown-op` (an `op` outside the fourteen), and `recovery-blocked` (a half-landed operation on a path this request touches, below under the recovery contract). The last is not "later": it stays until someone corrects a file by hand.
3. **The request shapes, by fixture name.** The eight operations that now answer keep the shapes FJ00 pinned: `codec/fixtures/valid/protocol/create.json`, `claim.json`, `release.json`, `set-mode.json`, `set-dependencies.json`, `adopt-plan.json`, `attach-evidence.json`, `reconcile.json`. Every widening is additive and pinned by a valid and an invalid fixture: `create.narrative.content`, an optional string (`valid/protocol/create-with-content.json`, `invalid/protocol/create-content-not-string.json`); `adopt-plan.role`, `"spec" | "plan"`, absent meaning `plan` (`adopt-plan-role-spec.json`, `adopt-plan-role-unknown.json`); `transition.payload.deferral`, `null` or the deferral object item 13 described and Prior confirmed (`transition-decision-deferred.json`, `transition-deferral-without-ruler.json`); `answer_ref` widened from `record_ref` to the common `reference` (`transition-decision-answered-by-citation.json`) and `implementation_ref` from `git_commit` to `git_commit | reference` (no fixture of its own), both matching `record.schema.json`'s `decision_control`; `record_selector.path` admits `*.evidence.json` (`show-evidence.json`). In `record.schema.json` the deferral object moved to `$defs/deferral` with no property order changed, so every decision fixture serialises to the same bytes. The manifest's nine notes on the formerly deferred operations' fixtures lost their closing "Answered operation-unknown/not-implemented-in-fj01." (notes are never asserted); `migration`'s says it lands in FJ04.
4. **`transition` over every kind.** Package as in FJ01, with the response shape byte-identical; issue (`disposition`); plan (the state only: steps and criteria are request 18); discussion (the state); decision (`answer_ref`, `implementation_ref`, `superseded_by`, `deferral`, each resolved when it is a `record_ref` or `artefact_ref`, carried unresolved when it is a legacy citation or a foreign reference). Every kind answers the one shape `{operation_id, path, from, to, revision, previous_revision}` with `revisions`. An evidence file is `conflict/evidence-immutable`.
5. **`claim`, `release`, `set-mode`.** `claim` and `release` are `transition` with defaults and clearer refusals, through the same plan function (Prior's `## Claim/release decision for FJ02`, option 1): a `claim` and a `transition` to `claimed` with the same claim produce byte-identical records and the same revision, and refuse in the same class. A transition into `claimed` whose claim carries `claimed_at: null` is refused `schema-invalid/claimed-at-required` on both routes, so no less-checked `transition` bypass remains; a package already claimed is `conflict/already-claimed` through `claim` and `conflict` (`no edge claimed -> claimed`) through `transition`; `release` of a package not in `claimed` is `conflict/not-claimed`, a paused one included, since the table gives `paused → open` to `transition`. A package at rest in `claimed` with a null time, as an import may leave it, still moves out. `set-mode` answers `{operation_id, path, mode, revision, previous_revision}`; `autonomous` needs a `user-word` source whose `ref` resolves, or a resolving `record_ref`; a legacy source is refused (import only), `ordinary` with a source is refused, a terminal package is `conflict/package-terminal`. Ownership of a claim is not checked by the kernel: request 22.
6. **`create`, with and without `content`.** With `narrative.content` both halves of the pair are written in one journaled operation and the narrative must be absent (`conflict/narrative-exists`); without it the narrative must exist (`unresolved-reference/narrative-missing`) and the operation is one write. The kernel builds the record, never the payload alone: a package is `status: open`, `claim: null`, `mode {ordinary, null}`, with `origin` as sent and checked (a `package` origin must resolve to a package), so no route but `set-mode` with provenance makes a package `autonomous`; a package payload admits `domain` (required) and `references`, a record payload is the kind's initial `control`; new records take marker-free basenames. Refusals `conflict/id-in-use`, `conflict/record-exists`, `unknown-scope/store-kind-mismatch`. The answer is `{operation_id, path, kind, revision, narrative: {path, sha256}}`.
7. **`set-dependencies` and `adopt-plan`.** `set-dependencies` replaces `depends_on` after resolving every target to a package of this workbench and refusing self, duplicates and any cycle through the package (`conflict/cycle` naming it); conditions are evaluated by `reconcile`, not here. `adopt-plan` with `role: plan` replaces the package's plan entry, moves the replaced plan's reference into `references` and clears that plan record's `acceptance` (up to three files in one intent); with `role: spec` it adds a spec entry and sets the spec record's `acceptance`. The request's `revision` must equal the hash of the plan's narrative now (`conflict/plan-revision-mismatch`). The answer is `{operation_id, path, role, document {path, revision, narrative}, replaced, revision, previous_revision}`.
8. **The evidence selector and naming rule.** An evidence record is `<basename>.evidence.json` beside its report `<basename>.md` in a `reviews/` store, and its `report.path` must name exactly that neighbour (`unknown-scope/report-not-neighbour`). A record is immutable once accepted, so a correction over an unchanged report is `<basename>.<n>.evidence.json`, `n` a decimal from 2 without a leading zero; any other trailing segment belongs to the basename. `attach-evidence` and a package's `transition` to `done` bind evidence through one check: the record resolves by id at the bound revision, names its neighbour, whose bytes are at the named hash, belongs to this workbench, carries the binding's execution policy, and was produced against the package narrative as it stands (`missing-evidence/brief-changed`) and the plan in force (`plan-changed`, `no-active-plan`). `validate` gains findings for evidence files only, so `06-validate` still answers `checked: 3`.
9. **`reconcile` shows, and repairs nothing.** Its report is `{workbench, state, scope, checked, intents, records, references, evidence, dependencies, narratives}`: blocked intents with each file's state, every `validate` finding, every reference site (`resolved`, `unresolved`, `ambiguous`, `foreign`, `unchecked`), every evidence binding (`fresh` or the refusal reason), every `depends_on` edge (`satisfied`, `unmet`, cycles), and status copies or conflict markers in the narrative of a non-terminal record.

### The replay and recovery contract Prior's adapter relies on

- **The sequence.** Every mutation runs one order: workbench state; the workbench write lock; sweep of transient entries; recovery of every pending intent; the replay lookup; the operation's plan function (reads, `expected_revision` against the stored bytes, the operation's rules, schema validation of every record it would write); the durable intent; each write by temp file, fsync and rename; the stored answer; the intent's removal.
- **The intent is the commit point.** It is the directory `.json-state/journal/<operation_id>/`, built under a dot name and committed by one rename. Before the rename nothing is written and nothing stored, so a retry is an ordinary first attempt. After it the operation lands, on this attempt or through recovery on a later one.
- **An unknown outcome is resolved by any later request.** When the adapter returns `ErrCodecOutcomeUnknown` (killed, deadline, malformed output), the next mutation of any kind recovers every pending intent under the lock before its own work, and a read that finds a pending intent whose files are all at pre or post takes the lock and recovers before it reads. A `show` therefore tells the host which state holds, and an identical retry returns the stored answer when the first attempt passed the commit point or runs as a first attempt when it did not. Whether the adapter retries on its own stays Prior's rule ("uncertain process outcomes without automatic retry"); the codec makes a `show` and a deliberate identical retry both safe.
- **Identical retry answered, divergent refused.** Replay equality is FJ01's comparison of the key-sorted request, now held as a `sha256:` digest: the same `operation_id` and the same request answer the stored response byte for byte; the same id with any other request is `conflict/operation-id-reused` and writes nothing. The digest covers every field, `workbench` included, so a workbench copied to another path answers a replay of an old id `operation-id-reused`, not the stored answer.
- **Fresh id against the old revision refused.** A new `operation_id` sent with the revision the first attempt started from is `conflict/revision-mismatch` once that attempt landed, so nothing runs twice.
- **`recovery-blocked` and what it means.** Recovery reads each file an intent names by three hashes, post first: at post it has landed, at pre (or absent where it is to be created) it has not and the staged bytes are written, at neither a hand edit or a pull intervened. A live writer's files are only ever at pre or post. A file at neither blocks the intent: the kernel leaves file and intent exactly as they stand and never overwrites either. A request that reads or writes a path a blocked intent names is `operation-unknown/recovery-blocked` naming the intent and the diverged path; a request reusing the blocked intent's id is `recovery-blocked` when it is the same request (the stored response would report a state the files do not hold) and `conflict/operation-id-reused` when it is not. Two hand corrections clear it, and the kernel performs neither: restore the file to its pre-bytes, after which the next request rolls forward; or delete the intent directory and accept the writes that had landed. `reconcile` lists the per-file state.
- **The read protocol.** A read takes no lock unless a writer may be mid-flight: it lists `journal/` then `ops/`, ignoring dot-named entries, reads, lists both again in that order, and retries on a difference. A blocked intent is classified without the lock and is a stable member of both listings, so it no longer sends every later read through the lock. A read that must wait for a live writer can reach the host's deadline before the codec's own `conflict/lock-timeout`; it has no effect either way.
- **Local state, not protocol.** The stored answer is `.json-state/ops/<operation_id>.json` as `{operation_id, op, request_digest, response}`; an answer the FJ01 bundle stored (`{operation_id, request, response}`) is still read, by digesting its `request`. No file under `.json-state/` carries a request body. `ops/` is not pruned (request 21).

### The lock

- **One file for the whole workbench:** `.json-state/write.lock`, content `pid: <n>\nhost: <hostname>\nnonce: <random>\nacquired_at: <iso>\n`, created by linking a complete file to the name, so every lock this codec writes records its PID. FJ01's per-record `.json-state/<sha256 of the record path>.lock` is neither written nor read by the FJ02 bundle.
- **A dead holder on the same host is replaced at once.** A lock of this host, or one recording no host (FJ01's format), is stale the moment its PID is dead, with no age condition, and live while the PID answers. A writer killed by the adapter's SIGKILL of the process group (`codec_process.go` line 108) no longer stalls the workbench for 60 s: the next request replaces its lock and lands. Only a lock recording no PID at all is stale by age, after 60 s. A stale lock is never unlinked: the one waiter that creates the takeover claim for that instance (`write.lock.takeover.<hash>`) renames its own lock over the name, so no waiter removes a lock it did not judge.
- **Another host's lock never.** A PID means nothing off the host that recorded it; the waiter answers `conflict/lock-timeout` after its wait (65 s by default), naming the holder and its host, and such a lock is removed by hand once its holder is known to be gone.
- **The live-owner behaviour Prior tests is unchanged:** a live PID keeps the codec waiting whatever the lock's age, and the lock's bytes and the record stay as they were. Fusion pins it twice, `codec/src/__tests__/store.test.ts` and through the spawned bundle in `kernel.test.ts`, each with FJ01's content, a live PID and an hour-old mtime.
- **Prior's regression moves.** `TestCodecDoesNotReapAnOldLockWhoseOwnerIsAlive` writes its hand-made lock at `.json-state/<hex of sha256("work-packages/260928-1200-parser-fix/package.json")>.lock` (`codec_process_test.go` lines 310 and 311). Against the FJ02 bundle that file is no lock: the transition takes `write.lock`, lands, and the test's record check fails. The path becomes `filepath.Join(c.Workbench, ".json-state", "write.lock")`, and the `key` line goes. The content should take the FJ02 form (`pid` of the test process, `host` from `os.Hostname()`, a `nonce`, `acquired_at`), so the regression pins the format the codec writes; FJ01's two-line content is still read as this host's and would keep the test green too. The request, deadline and assertions stay. The codec also writes `.json-state/.gitignore` (`*`) before it takes any lock; the test does not enumerate the directory, so that file moves nothing.
- **One bundle per workbench.** An FJ01 bundle and an FJ02 bundle writing one workbench at once exclude nothing between them, since each takes a lock the other does not read. Prior pins one digest per adapter and the Claude side has no consumer before FJ03, so no host runs two; the re-pin moves every Prior caller to the new digest at once.

### The FJ02 recorded session

`codec/fixtures/protocol-session-fj02/` holds fifteen exchanges through `bin/fusion-record` over a fresh copy of `codec/fixtures/workbench/`, in this order: `01-create` (a package with its body), `02-claim`, `03-release`, `04-claim`, `05-set-mode` (autonomous, a `user-word` source), `06-create` (a plan record with its body into the package's container), `07-adopt-plan`, `08-set-dependencies` (on the scratch open package, naming the new one), `09-attach-evidence`, `10-transition` (the plan to `in_progress`), `11-transition` (the package to `done`, binding the evidence), `12-create` (01 repeated: byte-identical to `01-create.response.json`), `13-create` (01's id with another body: `conflict/operation-id-reused`), `14-show` (the shared issue, recovered from a seeded pending intent), `15-reconcile`. Three exchanges need files no operation writes; each set sits under `seed/<nn>-<op>/`, laid out as the workbench is, and is copied onto the workbench root immediately before its exchange and not earlier: `seed/05-set-mode/` (the memo the mode cites), `seed/09-attach-evidence/` (the evidence record and its report), `seed/14-show/` (one pending intent directory under `.json-state/journal/`, in the step 2 format). The one substitution is FJ01's: the literal `<workbench>` in every request, and in the `15-reconcile` answer, the only one that echoes the root. Every id, claim time and the intent's `created_at` are fixed literals, and no answer carries a lock's host, PID or nonce, so the bytes are the same on every machine with this bundle; they were byte-stable across three recorder runs with different temp roots and a shell replay. To replay: copy the scratch workbench to W; for each exchange in order, copy its `seed/` set onto W first when one exists (`cp -R seed/<nn>-<op>/. W/`), substitute W for `<workbench>` in the request, write it to `node codec/dist/fusion-record.js`, substitute `<workbench>` for W in stdout and compare byte for byte; exit is 0 for all fifteen. Start from a fresh W for every run. `codec/fixtures/protocol-session-fj02/README.md` is the procedure in full, with what `15-reconcile` reports on purpose (the scratch `done` package's dangling plan and evidence, the parser-fix narrative's `**Status:** open` line).

Seven things are asked, in the order they are needed.

### 18. The operation that moves a plan's steps and criteria

**Closes:** the open question in the FJ02 plan (`260928-2251_*_plan-fj02-operation-kernel-revisions-and-local-transactions.md`, `## Open Questions`), and the gap in section 6's operation table of `Prior: concept/fusion-json-workbench-spec.md`, which names no operation for a plan's `steps` and `criteria`. FJ03's consumers need one: an implementer marks steps done, and today only `transition` of the plan's `state` exists. Preferred form: a reply, confirming the proposal or naming the alternative.

The fusion proposal: `transition` on a plan record admits `payload.steps` and `payload.criteria`, either alongside a state change or with `to` equal to the current state, and each step's move is checked by `stepAllowed` against the step states and edges `codec/contract/transitions.json` already carries for plans. It adds no operation to the protocol's fourteen and runs through the same kernel, CAS and replay. The alternative is a named operation of its own in section 6's table, which changes the protocol enum, the spec and a fixture pair. Either is additive; FJ03 does not plan the consumer before the answer.

### 19. How a Claude-side reviewer writes an evidence record

**Closes:** the gap `codec/README.md` `## Evidence records on disk` names ("No FJ02 operation writes an evidence record"), which is why `seed/09-attach-evidence/` exists as copied files; and, on the Prior side, whether Prior's own evidence reaches the workbench through the kernel, as `## Remaining integration`'s one-writer sentence reads. Preferred form: a reply.

The fusion proposal: `create` admits `kind: evidence`, with the record body as payload, as one journaled write; the control path follows from the record's `report.path` by the naming rule (item 8 above), the report must already be on disk at the named hash, and an existing record under the name is `conflict/record-exists`, since evidence is immutable and a correction takes `<basename>.<n>.evidence.json`. Asked of Prior: whether this route suits Prior's own evidence writer as well, or Prior needs a different one.

### 20. Confirm `adopt-plan`'s `role` and the deferral payload on `transition`

**Closes:** the `adopt-plan` branch's `role` and the `transition` payload's `deferral`, `answer_ref` and `implementation_ref` in `codec/schemas/protocol.schema.json`, and the `adopt-plan` and `transition` rows of section 6 of `Prior: concept/fusion-json-workbench-spec.md`, whose wording names neither a spec binding nor a deferral payload. Preferred form: a reply, confirm or correct; a correction is a schema change under the freeze rule (additive, a new bundle digest, a re-pin under requests 16 and 17).

As applied: `role: spec` binds a spec (`active_documents[].role: spec`, any number, distinct by record id) and sets its `acceptance`; `role: plan`, the default, replaces the one plan. The deferral payload is the object item 13 described and Prior's FJ01b response confirmed, by reference to one definition, `record.schema.json` `$defs/deferral`; `answer_ref` and `implementation_ref` admit what the record admits.

### 21. The retention of `.json-state/ops/`

**Closes:** the retention of stored answers, unbounded in FJ02 (`codec/README.md` `## The kernel and the journal`). Preferred form: a reply.

`ops/` grows by one small file per landed mutation and is never pruned. A revision is the hash of the stored bytes, so content revisions return: after a `claim` and then a `release` a package stands at the bytes and the revision it had before the claim, and a replayed old operation whose answer was pruned passes the CAS and lands again. Any future pruning needs durable protection against old operation ids first. [This paragraph was corrected at FJ02b after Prior's response 21; the fusion test that pins the statement is `codec/src/__tests__/kernel.test.ts`, `describe("stored answers are never pruned: content revisions return")`.] Pruning is not free for reads either: the read protocol accepts a lock-free read when the id set of `journal/` and `ops/` is equal before and after, which holds because `ops/` only grows, so a bound means pruning under the lock and a change to how reads compare. Asked: does Prior want a bound (by age, by count, or tied to a campaign's lifetime), and by when? Without an answer `ops/` stays unbounded through FJ03.

### 22. Claim ownership for `release` and every `transition` out of `claimed`

**Closes:** claim C16 of the closed discussion `260929-0709_*_fj02-kernel-plan-and-three-open-choices.md`, priced by its claim C23; the ownership comment in `release`'s plan function in `codec/src/cli/ops.ts`; and the precondition in the FJ02 plan's `## Where this work stops` that FJ03 does not start before this answer or names its absence. Preferred form: a reply naming one of the two readings.

Prior's `## Claim/release decision for FJ02` (`Prior: docs/design/fusion-fj01-prior-response.md`) lists "authorizer checks, checkout/person binding, claim ownership, allowed source/target, evidence, expected revision and durable operation-ID binding" as checks that "must apply to both entry points through the same kernel", and the FJ01b response keeps that. The same list opens with authorizer checks, which run on the host (`codec_process.go`), so "through the same kernel" can mean the same route or the kernel doing the check. The fusion side cannot decide which. Question: for `release` and for every `transition` out of `claimed` (to `open`, `paused`, `done` or `dropped`), is the condition met

- **(a)** when the host decides ownership from `show` and sends that record's revision as `expected_revision`, so the write lands only against the record the host inspected, which is what FJ02 implements; or
- **(b)** only when the kernel compares the standing claim against an identity carried in the request?

The price of (b): the `transition` and `release` request shapes carry no checkout field (`protocol.schema.json`, their two branches), and the only identity the kernel receives is `actor.person`, which `common.schema.json` defines as "Attribution, never authorisation" and which is nullable, so a kernel check needs a new request field. Made mandatory, that field changes the answer to Prior's recorded handback: `codec/fixtures/prior-handback/transition.request.json` moves `claimed → paused` and carries none. Made optional, it leaves a less-checked route, which Prior's ruling excludes. Either way (b) is a change to step 4 of the FJ02 plan and to the request shapes, planned before FJ03 rather than inside it. FJ02 keeps (a) until Prior answers, and FJ03 does not start before the answer or names its absence in its own head.

FJ03 is planned against this revision, `72a7051b` and the bundle digest above, and the answers to requests 18, 19 and 22.

## FJ02b (the two approved kernel extensions)

**Written against:** fusion commit `6e01977f` on branch `fj-json-workbench` (2026-09-29), which read Prior at commit `38acd95` (`Prior: docs/design/fusion-fj02-prior-response.md`, and section 6 of `Prior: concept/fusion-json-workbench-spec.md`, whose operation table names both extensions as confirmed). `codec/dist/fusion-record.js` at that commit is 524 930 bytes, `sha256:5f116c6436175a6b8e1cb08aa2625d2e2f68ef193657b00eb1891ea8d3b965bd`. The bundle took those bytes at `ab3f87e2` (step 3, the last commit that moved code or an inlined file); steps 4 and 5 added tests, the recorded session and README text and moved no inlined file.

FJ02b implements the two extensions Prior approved in its FJ02 response, plan progress through `transition` (response 18) and `create` of an evidence record (response 19), corrects the retention claim that response 21 refuted, and freezes the result at one revision before FJ03. Both extensions are plan functions over the kernel FJ02 landed, so the sequence, the replay, the CAS, the journal, recovery, the lock and the read protocol stated under `## FJ02` apply to them unchanged, and none of it was written a second time. The commits, in order: `fe4ff899` (steps 1 and 2, the schema widenings with their fixtures and plan progress, committed together because the widened payload schema without its plan function would have let a state-changing plan transition land without applying its steps), `ab3f87e2` (3, evidence creation), `c4246ef4` (4, the retention regression), `6e01977f` (5, the recorded session and the codec README). The suite and its committed-bundle gate were green at every one of the four, so the one intermediate digest (`fe4ff899`: 519 932 bytes, `sha256:ed46655200617eea5e2fa491b3c8d47a4485b5dc291b465cff451044516488bd`) is a real revision, and this section names the last. Nothing here touched `codec/contract/`, the 13 goldens, the six pairs under `codec/fixtures/protocol-session/`, the fifteen exchanges, the `seed/` and the README under `codec/fixtures/protocol-session-fj02/`, the scratch workbench under `codec/fixtures/workbench/`, the handback under `codec/fixtures/prior-handback/`, `codec/package.json` or `bin/fusion-record`: each is byte-identical to `72a7051b`, and so to `9764be4c`, the commit Prior pinned. At `6e01977f`, `cd codec && CODEC_REQUIRE_GOLDENS=1 npm test` is green: 17 test files, 1 043 tests, `fixtures: 226 manifest entries`, `0 derived-from-source, 13 Go-emitted golden(s)`, `163 of 163 prior_keys exercised`, with `round-trip-cli.test.ts`, `round-trip-cli-fj02.test.ts`, `round-trip-cli-fj02b.test.ts` and `prior-handback.test.ts` without regeneration; `npm run typecheck` exits 0. `codec/README.md` (`## The CLI`, `## The kernel and the journal`, `## Evidence records on disk`) is the full statement; what follows is what the Prior side needs from it.

This text is committed after `6e01977f`, as the FJ02 text was committed at `9764be4c` after `72a7051b`. It changes `codec/fixtures/prior/REQUESTS.md` and no other file under `codec/` or `bin/`, which `git diff --stat 6e01977f <the commit Prior pins> -- codec bin` shows.

### What landed

1. **The request shapes, by fixture name.** Every schema change is additive, and each is pinned by a valid and an invalid fixture. In `codec/schemas/protocol.schema.json` the `transition` payload gains `steps` and `criteria`, two optional arrays whose items are `record.schema.json` `$defs/plan_step` (`{id, state}`, `state` one of `open`, `in_progress`, `done`) and `$defs/plan_criterion` (`{id, met}`, `met` a boolean or `null`): `valid/protocol/transition-plan-progress.json`, `invalid/protocol/transition-plan-step-state-unknown.json` (a step `state: "blocked"`), `invalid/protocol/transition-plan-criterion-met-string.json` (`met: "yes"`). Evidence creation is a second `oneOf` branch of `create`, `{op, workbench?, operation_id, id, kind: "evidence", scope: {container, store: "reviews"}, payload}`, where `payload` is the complete record by reference to `fusion.evidence/v1`: `valid/protocol/create-evidence.json` (`predecessor: null`), `valid/protocol/create-evidence-correction.json` (a `record_ref` predecessor), `invalid/protocol/create-evidence-without-scope.json`, `invalid/protocol/create-evidence-payload-not-evidence.json` (the payload lacks `report`), `invalid/protocol/create-evidence-with-narrative.json` (a `narrative`, which neither branch admits beside `kind: evidence`). The record branch of `create` is not edited: its `kind` enum lacks `evidence` and the new branch's `kind` is the const `evidence`, so the two are disjoint and every request that validated at `72a7051b` validates as before. In `record.schema.json` the step and criterion item definitions moved into `$defs` with no property order changed, so every plan fixture and every plan record serialises to the same bytes. No fixture that existed at `72a7051b` changed; the manifest gained the eight entries.
2. **Plan progress through `transition` (request 18).** No fifteenth operation. A `transition` on a plan record reads `payload.steps` and `payload.criteria` as updates keyed by the `id` of an entry the stored plan already has. The checks run in this order after the CAS, and each refuses with nothing written. First, `steps` or `criteria` sent for a record that is not a plan is `schema-invalid/payload-field-not-admitted`. Second, the state: when `to` differs from the stored state, or the payload carries neither field, the move is the table's edge exactly as at FJ02; when `to` equals the stored state and the payload carries progress, a terminal plan (`closed`, `deferred`) is `conflict/transition-refused`. Third, the ids: an id repeated in the payload, or in the stored array the payload updates, is `schema-invalid/duplicate-step-id` or `duplicate-criterion-id`, and an id the stored plan lacks is `unresolved-reference/unknown-step-id` or `unknown-criterion-id`. Fourth, each step whose `state` differs from the stored one moves along the step edges of `codec/contract/transitions.json` (`open → in_progress`, `in_progress → done`, `open → done`) through `stepAllowed`; anything else, `done → open` included, is `conflict/transition-refused` with the detail naming the step. An entry equal to the stored one is admitted and changes nothing. Fifth, a criterion takes any `met` the schema admits, in any direction. Sixth, with `to` equal to the stored state a payload that changes no value is `conflict/transition-refused`: the exception is for progress and is no general self-transition. Seventh, the result is the stored arrays with the named entries replaced in place; the order, every entry left out, `acceptance` and the record's `references` are untouched, the whole record is validated as before, and the answer keeps FJ02's shape `{operation_id, path, from, to, revision, previous_revision}` with `revisions`. The progress-only route and the state-changing route run through the same plan function, so scope, CAS and replay are the same on both. One property the approval did not name: a stored array that repeats an id refuses only a request that updates that array, so a plan move without progress lands on such a record as it did at FJ02. `codec/src/__tests__/ops.test.ts`, `describe("transition: plan progress")`, pins this in 16 cases.
3. **`create` refuses a repeated id on a new plan.** An update keyed by id is only well defined over unique ids, and `create` is the one writer of a new plan, so a plan payload whose `steps` or `criteria` repeat an id is `schema-invalid/duplicate-step-id` or `duplicate-criterion-id`, checked after the initial-state check, with nothing written. This is the one place where a request that landed at `72a7051b` is now refused. No recorded exchange and no fixture repeats an id.
4. **`create` of `kind: evidence` (request 19).** It is the sole write route for a new evidence record, for both hosts. The report is the reviewer's Markdown file and is already on disk; the kernel writes one file, the payload serialised in the schema's property order, and adds nothing to it. The checks run in this order, and each refuses with nothing written: the payload's `id` is the envelope's (`schema-invalid/id-mismatch`); the payload's `workbench_id` is this workbench's (`unknown-scope/foreign-workbench-id`); the report's directory is the scope's `reviews/` store (`unknown-scope/store-kind-mismatch`) and the scope's container is a package directory (`unknown-scope/container-missing`); the report's basename is marker-free, ends in `.md`, and `<basename>.evidence.json` reads back as a first record of that basename (`schema-invalid/report-name`; a basename ending in `.2` would read as a correction counter); the report is on disk at the hash the payload names (`unresolved-reference/report-missing`, `missing-evidence/report-changed`); the `id` is carried by no record of the workbench (`conflict/id-in-use`); the predecessor, when set, resolves to an evidence record of this workbench, at its pinned revision when one is given (an `unresolved-reference` refusal of the resolver, `unresolved-reference/not-evidence`, `missing-evidence/evidence-revision-mismatch`); a predecessor naming the same `report.path` names the same `report.sha256` as the payload (`conflict/predecessor-report-changed`); no file stands at the chosen path (`conflict/record-exists`). The answer is `{operation_id, path, kind: "evidence", revision, report: {path, sha256}}` with `revisions` naming the one file: `create`'s shape with the report in place of the narrative, and `path` the file the kernel chose. `ops.test.ts`, `describe("create: evidence")`, pins this in 10 cases.
5. **The path, and what the counter guarantees.** A record without a predecessor, or whose predecessor names another report, is the report's first record, `<basename>.evidence.json`; a file standing there is `conflict/record-exists` and is never read as a correction. A predecessor naming the same report at the same hash makes a correction, `<basename>.<n>.evidence.json`, with `n` one above the highest counter present in the directory as it stands, and 2 when none is present. The counter is read under the write lock and frozen in the intent and the stored answer, so an identical retry answers the same path after a later correction has landed, and the chosen path never collides with a file that stands. That is the whole guarantee. No durable counter is kept: a suffix freed by a hand deletion of the highest file can be chosen again, while a gap below the highest is not refilled. A correction never modifies the predecessor record or the report.
6. **Labels confer nothing.** Nothing in creation reads `host`, `execution_policy` or `verdict`. The kernel receives no caller identity it may authorise on, so `host: prior`, `prior-enforced` and `accept` are stored as sent and nothing is derived from them; `attach-evidence` and a package's move to `done` check policy agreement and freshness on their own, as at FJ02, and a `claude-guided` record stays `claude-guided` in its bytes. Whether a label is genuine is the host's to establish (response 19).
7. **The test helper is on the real route.** `codec/src/__tests__/helpers/seed.ts` writes the report as a plain file and creates the record through `create` with `kind: evidence`; no test writes an evidence record through a plan function of its own. `round-trip-cli-fj02.test.ts` is green without regeneration, which proves that `codec/fixtures/protocol-session-fj02/seed/09-attach-evidence/` is byte-identical to what the real route writes. A second helper places a pair by hand, with no check, and stands for a record that arrived by hand edit or by a pull; only the cases whose record `create` refuses use it.
8. **Retention (request 21).** `ops/` is never pruned. `codec/src/__tests__/kernel.test.ts`, `describe("stored answers are never pruned: content revisions return")`, is the fusion twin of Prior's `TestCodecFJ02RetainedAnswerPreventsABAReplay`, in 4 cases through the committed bundle over the recorded `01-create`, `02-claim` and `03-release` of the FJ02 session: after `03` the package is back at the revision `01` answered; with the answer retained, the replayed `02` answers the recorded bytes and the package stays `open`; with only `02`'s stored answer deleted on a scratch copy, the replayed `02` passes the CAS and lands a second time; and a `release` under a fresh operation id against `02`'s revision is `conflict/revision-mismatch`, so the CAS is intact and the defect is the return of old bytes. Request 21 above and `codec/README.md` `## The kernel and the journal` now say so, and both say that any future pruning needs durable protection against old operation ids and a revised read protocol first.
9. **What did not change.** `inspect` over the scratch workbench answers the same bytes through the bundle of `72a7051b` and the bundle of `6e01977f`: the thirteen implemented operations, `migration` deferred, the six kinds. `inspect` therefore does not announce the two extensions, and a host knows them by the bundle digest it pinned. The reason `payload-field-not-admitted` existed on `create` and is new on `transition`; the reasons new with this revision are `duplicate-step-id`, `duplicate-criterion-id`, `unknown-step-id`, `unknown-criterion-id`, `id-mismatch`, `report-name` and `predecessor-report-changed`. Every other refusal reuses a reason FJ02 already answered.

### The FJ02b recorded session

`codec/fixtures/protocol-session-fj02b/` holds twenty exchanges through `bin/fusion-record` over a fresh copy of `codec/fixtures/workbench/`, a README and `seed/`. It is a third session and no addition to the FJ02 session, so the fifteen exchanges Prior replayed stay byte-identical and Prior's pin of them stays valid. The order: `01-create` (a package P with its body), `02-create` (a plan L with its body into P's container, steps `s1`, `s2`, `s3` each `open`, criteria `c1`, `c2` each `null`), `03-transition` (L `open → in_progress`, the payload moving `s1` to `in_progress`), `04-transition` (L `in_progress → in_progress`, moving `s1` to `done`, `s2` to `in_progress` and `c1` to `true`), `05-transition` (04 repeated: byte-identical to `04-transition.response.json`), `06-show` (L: `s3` still `open`, `c2` still `null`, the stored order kept), `07-transition` (progress only against the revision 03 returned: `conflict/revision-mismatch`), `08-transition` (`s1` back to `open`: `conflict/transition-refused`), `09-transition` (the payload naming `s2` twice: `schema-invalid/duplicate-step-id`), `10-transition` (the payload naming `s9`: `unresolved-reference/unknown-step-id`), `11-create` (evidence E1 for P over the report R, `predecessor: null`: the first-record path), `12-create` (E2 correcting E1: counter 2), `13-create` (E3 correcting E1: counter 3), `14-create` (12 repeated after 13 landed: byte-identical to `12-create.response.json`, so counter 2 and not 4), `15-show` (E1 at the revision 11 returned, unchanged by the two corrections), `16-create` (E4 with `predecessor: null` over R: `conflict/record-exists`), `17-create` (E5 naming a hash R does not have: `missing-evidence/report-changed`), `18-transition` (L `in_progress → closed`), `19-transition` (progress only on the closed L: `conflict/transition-refused`), `20-create` (E6 correcting E1 after the report was replaced, the payload naming the new hash: `conflict/predecessor-report-changed`). Two exchanges need a file no operation writes, the report; each set sits under `seed/<nn>-<op>/`, laid out as the workbench is, and is copied onto the workbench root immediately before its exchange and not earlier: `seed/11-create/` (R, 119 bytes, `sha256:9c3bb53658871186f4a4e74a5da93ee88cecfa716f9672b0d03cc19ff64b9554`) and `seed/20-create/` (R at the same path with other bytes, 165 bytes, `sha256:cbcdf898be49e6224bfd7298d6f679690a3f7c116497e5bb2355615ae06ab8d5`). `20-create` is last because its seed changes the report that 11 to 17 are judged against. Unlike the FJ02 session, no evidence record is seeded: E1, E2 and E3 are written by the kernel. The one substitution is FJ01's: the literal `<workbench>` in the `workbench` field of every request. No answer of the twenty echoes the root, so no response file carries the placeholder. Every id and every time is a fixed literal, and no answer carries a lock's host, PID or nonce, so the bytes are the same on every machine with this bundle: `round-trip-cli-fj02b.test.ts` passes 49 of its 49 cases against the recorded files, and a shell replay through the bundle of `6e01977f`, over a scratch workbench whose path contains spaces and a comma, answered 20 of the 20 exchanges byte for byte with exit 0. To replay: copy the scratch workbench to W; for each exchange in order, copy its `seed/` set onto W first when one exists (`cp -R seed/<nn>-<op>/. W/`), substitute W for `<workbench>` in the request, write it to `node codec/dist/fusion-record.js`, and compare stdout byte for byte. Start from a fresh W for every run. After 20 the three landed evidence records name R at a hash R no longer has, which `validate` and `reconcile` would report; the session sends neither. `codec/fixtures/protocol-session-fj02b/README.md` is the procedure in full.

### Recovery and rollout consequences

The fusion side has read `## Recovery and rollout consequences` of Prior's FJ02 response and acknowledges its three statements. A read may finish a committed intent, a multi-file one included, so a read is no promise of zero physical writes, and a host authorises that recovery apart from a read-only role. The FJ01 and FJ02 lock paths are incompatible, so an FJ01 writer must not resume beside a later one on the same workbench. One compatible codec pin is active per workbench, activated after old writers are quiescent. FJ02b changes nothing in any of the three: it adds no kind of file under `.json-state/`, no lock and no read path, and `codec/src/kernel.ts`, `codec/src/store.ts` and the journal are the files of `72a7051b`, since `codec/src/cli/ops.ts` and `codec/src/cli/protocol.ts` are the only two source files outside the tests that moved. `codec/README.md` `## The kernel and the journal` now carries the first statement in Prior's terms. FJ03's consumers on the Claude side must honour all three.

Three items follow, in the order they are needed. Requests 23 and 24 ask for a re-snapshot and a re-pin; item 25 asks nothing.

### 27. Which write creates `workbench.json` for a new workbench: ruled

**Closes:** fusion decision `260929-1810_*_which-write-creates-the-manifest-of-a-new-json-controlled-workbench.md`, the `initialize` row of section 6 and the "Neuanlage" paragraph of section 4.1 of the specification, both at `ddd4973`. Preferred form: none needed for the ruling; the qualification it asks for arrives as a hand-over of its own.

The question: no operation of section 6 at `e3bc25b` writes a manifest. The four reads bootstrap nothing, `create` writes into a workbench that already is JSON-controlled, and `migration` activates a manifest as the last step of converting an existing one (FJ04). `/fusion:setup` is the only workflow that creates a workbench, and at `b4c8f7ca` it writes the store directories and `.fusion-setup` and no manifest. Once FJ03a's resolvers refuse everything that is not JSON-controlled, a fresh workbench is refused too, so on this branch `/fusion:setup` ends at its own path-resolution step until the operation exists. Fusion proposed a new codec operation (option 1) over a serialiser in the skill body, which would be a second writer of fusion JSON, and over `migration` covering it, which would be a migration with nothing to migrate.

Ruled in `## 27. New manifest: a codec-owned initialize operation`, same file and commit: option 1. `initialize` joins the shared codec contract; `/fusion:setup` requests it on an existing empty target directory before it writes stores, marker and assets, and Prior uses the same operation when its host provisions a workbench, so neither host writes manifest JSON itself. It takes a caller-supplied workbench UUID and a stable `operation_id` and writes `fusion.workbench/v1` with `required_features: ["json-control-v1"]`, `migration: null` and `extensions: {}`; the answer carries the identity, the manifest path and the byte revision. A manifest already present, any existing content (legacy Markdown without JSON included) or a target that is no directory is refused by name; an already JSON-controlled workbench is inspected and reused by Setup and not initialised again under a new id; a legacy one goes through FJ04. The same lock, atomic activation, retained replay answer and committed-intent recovery apply: an identical replay returns the recorded answer after records have since been added, the same id with other input is a conflict, and divergent recovery never overwrites; only the initialiser's own validated lock and recovery metadata are exempt from the empty-directory check. What fusion owes before FJ03c, as an additive kernel change with a plan of its own: the schema and the typed refusal fixtures; tests for a non-empty legacy target, an existing manifest, a non-directory target, competing initialisers, replay after later writes, a changed request, interruption before and after the commit point, and divergent recovery; and a recorded session for Prior, with the reason names pinned in that hand-over. Prior then re-pins and runs conformance, and FJ03c depends on that qualification. FJ03a's bundle stays the one above.

### 28. `list` on a legacy workbench answers an empty list and names no state

**Closes:** fusion issue `260929-1810_*_list-answers-a-legacy-workbench-with-an-empty-list-and-names-no-state.md`, and the sentence its acceptance asks for in `codec/README.md` `## The CLI`, which today states no gate. Preferred form: a reply naming one of the two closures below, and saying whether Prior's adapter already sends `inspect` before it lists.

The measurement, taken at `b4c8f7ca` and re-taken on 2026-09-29 through the bundle above over a scratch directory holding `.fusion-setup`, one v12 package directory with its Markdown record, and no manifest: `{"op":"list"}` answers `{"ok":true,"result":{"workbench":"…","scope":null,"records":[]}}` and carries no `state`; `{"op":"validate"}` answers `state: "legacy", checked: 0, valid: true`; `{"op":"inspect"}` answers `state: "legacy"`, `id: null`, `manifest: null`. Exit 0 on all three, and no `.json-state/` is created. The code is `list` in `codec/src/cli/ops.ts`, which builds its result from `workbench`, `scope` and `records` alone. A caller that lists without inspecting reads a legacy workbench as an empty one, which for a scope resolver means "nothing claimed" and a silent fall to the shared store, the failure row 1 of section 7 forbids ("niemals still nach shared ausweichen"). No recorded exchange of the three sessions sends `list`, so no pinned byte depends on the shape.

What fusion applies meanwhile, since a changed answer moves the digest both hosts pinned: the Claude side gates on `inspect`. `gate(workbench)` in `hooks/lib/record-client.ts` (fusion `16a6387e`) sends `inspect` before any other request and hands back the workbench id on `json-control`, and otherwise the state by name (`legacy`, or `unsupported` with the codec's diagnosis) or `refused` or `unanswered`. Every FJ03a consumer asks the gate first and proceeds only on `json-control`, and a `refused` or `unanswered` answer is never read as an empty store. `hooks/lib/__tests__/record-client.test.ts` pins the gate on a JSON-controlled, a legacy and an unsupported workbench and with the bundle absent.

Two closures are possible. (a) `list` carries `state` as `validate` and `reconcile` do: an additive change to the result, one fixture pair, a new digest and a re-pin. (b) The rule that a host gates on `inspect` before any other request is written into `codec/README.md` `## The CLI` and is the contract. The fusion proposal: (b) now, since it moves no byte, and (a) folded into the revision that `initialize` (item 27) brings, since that revision moves the digest anyway and a second re-pin for one field would cost Prior a conformance run of its own. Under either closure a test on the Claude side shows that a legacy workbench is never answered as "nothing claimed" (FJ03a step 3).

### 29. What row 2 of section 7 asks with "aktuelle aktive Dokumente nutzen"

**Closes:** row 2 of section 7 (`bin/fusion-paths`, `bin/fusion-rules`: "Bestehende Resolver-Schnittstellen erhalten, JSON-Scope und aktuelle aktive Dokumente nutzen") for FJ03a, and nothing on the Prior side unless the row means more than fusion reads into it.
